class="infobox-label">Predecessor</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Louis_I_of_Hungary" title="Louis I of Hungary">Louis</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Successor</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_II_Jagie%C5%82%C5%82o" title="Władysław II Jagiełło">Władysław II Jagiełło</a></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color: #e4dcf6;line-height:normal;padding:0.2em 0.2em"><div style="height: 4px; width:100%;"></div></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data">between <span style="display:none">(<span class="bday">1373-10-03</span>)</span>3 October 1373 and <span class="nowrap"><span style="display:none">(<span class="bday">1374-02-18</span>)</span>18 February 1374</span><br /><a href="/wiki/Buda" title="Buda">Buda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Hungary</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data"><time class="dday deathdate" datetime="1399-07-17">17 July 1399</time> (aged&#160;25)<br /><a href="/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w" title="Kraków">Kraków</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Poland_(1025%E2%80%931385)" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Poland (1025–1385)">Poland</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Burial</th><td class="infobox-data">24 August 1399<br /><div style="display:inline" class="label">Wawel Cathedral, Kraków</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_II_Jagie%C5%82%C5%82o" title="Władysław II Jagiełło">Władysław II Jagiełło</a> (Jogaila)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Issue_(genealogy)" title="Issue (genealogy)">Issue</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_Bonifacia" class="mw-redirect" title="Elizabeth Bonifacia">Elizabeth Bonifacia</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Dynasty" title="Dynasty">House</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Capetian_House_of_Anjou" title="Capetian House of Anjou">Capetian House of Anjou</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Father</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Louis_I_of_Hungary" title="Louis I of Hungary">Louis I of Hungary</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Mother</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_of_Bosnia" title="Elizabeth of Bosnia">Elizabeth of Bosnia</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religion</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Jadwiga</b> (<small>Polish:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="pl-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Polish" title="Help:IPA/Polish">[jadˈviɡa]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/5\/54\/Pl-Jadwiga.ogg\/Pl-Jadwiga.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Pl-Jadwiga.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/54/Pl-Jadwiga.ogg/Pl-Jadwiga.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Pl-Jadwiga.ogg" title="File:Pl-Jadwiga.ogg">i</a></sup>; 1373 or 1374&#160;&#8211;&#32;17 July 1399), also known as <b>Hedwig</b> (<a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">Hedvig</i>), was the first woman to be crowned as monarch of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Poland" title="Kingdom of Poland">Kingdom of Poland</a>. She reigned from 16 October 1384 until her death. She was the youngest daughter of <a href="/wiki/Louis_the_Great" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis the Great">Louis the Great</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/King_of_Poland" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Poland">Poland</a>, and his wife, <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_of_Bosnia" title="Elizabeth of Bosnia">Elizabeth of Bosnia</a>. Jadwiga was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Capetian_House_of_Anjou" title="Capetian House of Anjou">Capetian House of Anjou</a>, but she had more close forebears among the Polish <a href="/wiki/Piasts" class="mw-redirect" title="Piasts">Piasts</a> than among the Angevins.
</p><p>In 1375, it was planned that when becoming old enough, she would marry <a href="/wiki/William_of_Austria" class="mw-redirect" title="William of Austria">William of Austria</a> and she lived in Vienna from 1378 to 1380. Jadwiga's father is often thought to have regarded her and William as his favoured successors in Hungary after the 1378 death of her eldest sister, <a href="/wiki/Catherine_of_Hungary_(1370%E2%80%931378)" title="Catherine of Hungary (1370–1378)">Catherine</a>, since the following year the Polish nobility had pledged their homage to Louis' second daughter, <a href="/wiki/Mary,_Queen_of_Hungary" title="Mary, Queen of Hungary">Mary</a>, and Mary's fiancé, <a href="/wiki/Sigismund_of_Luxembourg" class="mw-redirect" title="Sigismund of Luxembourg">Sigismund of Luxembourg</a>. However, Louis died, and in 1382, at her mother's insistence, Mary was crowned "King of Hungary". Sigismund of Luxembourg tried to take control of Poland, but the Polish nobility countered that they would be obedient to a daughter of King Louis only if she settled in Poland.
</p><p>Queen Elizabeth then chose Jadwiga to reign in Poland, but did not send her to <a href="/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w" title="Kraków">Kraków</a> to be crowned. During the interregnum, <a href="/wiki/Siemowit_IV,_Duke_of_Masovia" title="Siemowit IV, Duke of Masovia">Siemowit IV, Duke of Masovia</a>, became a candidate for the Polish throne. The nobility of <a href="/wiki/Greater_Poland" title="Greater Poland">Greater Poland</a> favored him and proposed that he marry Jadwiga. However, <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Poland" title="Lesser Poland">Lesser Poland</a>'s nobility opposed him, and they persuaded Queen Elizabeth to send Jadwiga to Poland.
</p><p>Jadwiga was crowned king in Poland's capital, Kraków, on 16 October 1384. Her <a href="/wiki/Coronation" title="Coronation">coronation</a> either reflected the Polish nobility's opposition to her intended husband, William, becoming king without further negotiation, or simply, emphasized her status as <a href="/wiki/Queen_regnant" title="Queen regnant">the monarch</a>. With her mother's consent, Jadwiga's advisors opened negotiations with <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_II_Jagie%C5%82%C5%82o" title="Władysław II Jagiełło">Jogaila</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_Lithuanian_monarchs#House_of_Gediminas_(1285–1440)" title="List of Lithuanian monarchs">Grand Duke of Lithuania</a>, who was still a <a href="/wiki/Lithuanian_mythology" title="Lithuanian mythology">pagan</a>, concerning his potential marriage to Jadwiga. Jogaila signed the <a href="/wiki/Union_of_Krewo" title="Union of Krewo">Union of Krewo</a>, pledging to convert to Catholicism and to promote conversion of his pagan subjects. Meanwhile, William hastened to Kraków, hoping to marry his childhood fiancé, Jadwiga, but in late August 1385 the Polish nobles expelled him.
</p><p>Jogaila, who took the Catholic baptismal name Władysław, married Jadwiga on 15 February 1386. Legend says that she had agreed to marry him only after lengthy prayer, seeking <a href="/wiki/Divine_inspiration" title="Divine inspiration">divine inspiration</a>. Jogaila, now styled in Polish as, Władysław Jagiełło, was crowned King of Poland on 4 March 1386 as Jadwiga's co-ruler. Jogaila worked closely with his wife in that role. In any case, her real political power was limited.
</p><p>She remained passive when the rebellious nobles of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a>-<a href="/wiki/Croatia_in_personal_union_with_Hungary" title="Croatia in personal union with Hungary">Croatia</a> murdered her mother in early 1387. After that, Jadwiga marched into the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Galicia%E2%80%93Volhynia" title="Kingdom of Galicia–Volhynia">Kingdom of Galicia–Volhynia</a>, which had been under Hungarian rule, and persuaded most of the inhabitants to become subjects of the <a href="/wiki/Polish_Crown" class="mw-redirect" title="Polish Crown">Polish Crown</a>. She mediated between her husband's quarreling kin and between Poland and the <a href="/wiki/Teutonic_Order" title="Teutonic Order">Teutonic Order</a>.
</p><p>After her sister Mary died in 1395, Jadwiga and Jogaila laid claim to Hungary against the widowed <a href="/wiki/Sigismund,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Sigismund, Holy Roman Emperor">Sigismund of Luxembourg</a>, but the Hungarian lords did not support their claim and Sigismund easily retained his Hungarian throne. Jadwiga died four years later due to postpartum complications.
</p><p>In 1997, Jadwiga was <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span id="Childhood_.281373_or_1374_.E2.80.93_1382.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Childhood_(1373_or_1374_–_1382)">Childhood (1373 or 1374 – 1382)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Childhood (1373 or 1374 – 1382)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A lady and three girls pray on their knees before a bearded man" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg/220px-HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg/330px-HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg/440px-HAZU_77_17_lipnja_2008.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="586" /></a><figcaption>Jadwiga with her mother and sisters, as depicted on Saint Simeon's casket in <a href="/wiki/Zadar" title="Zadar">Zadar</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Jadwiga was born in <a href="/wiki/Buda" title="Buda">Buda</a>, the capital of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199156_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199156-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> She was the third and youngest daughter of <a href="/wiki/Louis_I_of_Hungary" title="Louis I of Hungary">Louis I</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/King_of_Poland" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Poland">Poland</a>, and his second wife, <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_of_Bosnia" title="Elizabeth of Bosnia">Elizabeth of Bosnia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWolf1993xliii_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWolf1993xliii-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Both her grandmothers were Polish princesses, connecting her to the native <a href="/wiki/Piast_dynasty" title="Piast dynasty">Piast dynasty</a> of <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199178_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199178-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Duczmal305_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Duczmal305-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Historian <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Halecki" class="mw-redirect" title="Oscar Halecki">Oscar Halecki</a> concluded that Jadwiga's "genealogical tree clearly shows that [she] had more Polish blood than any other".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199178_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199178-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> She was probably born between 3 October 1373 and 18 February 1374.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESroka199954–55_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESroka199954–55-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;nb 2&#93;</a></sup> She was named after her distant ancestor, Saint <a href="/wiki/Hedwig_of_Silesia" title="Hedwig of Silesia">Hedwig of Silesia</a>, who was especially venerated in the Hungarian royal court at the time of her birth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGromada1999434_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGromada1999434-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199189_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199189-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>King Louis, who had not fathered any sons, wanted to ensure the right of his daughters to inherit his realms.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies200590_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies200590-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Therefore, European royals regarded his three daughters as especially attractive brides.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169_4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Leopold_III,_Duke_of_Austria" title="Leopold III, Duke of Austria">Leopold III, Duke of Austria</a>, proposed his eldest son, <a href="/wiki/William,_Duke_of_Austria" title="William, Duke of Austria">William</a>, to Jadwiga already on 18 August 1374.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199158_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199158-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The envoys of the <a href="/wiki/Polish_nobles" class="mw-redirect" title="Polish nobles">Polish nobles</a> acknowledged that one of Louis's daughters would succeed him in Poland after he confirmed and extended their liberties in the <a href="/wiki/Privilege_of_Koszyce" title="Privilege of Koszyce">Privilege of Koszyce</a> on 17 September 1374.<sup id="cite_ref-Duczmal305_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Duczmal305-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies200590_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies200590-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESedlar199439–40_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESedlar199439–40-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> They took an oath of loyalty to Catherine on Louis's demand.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20158_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20158-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Louis agreed to give Jadwiga in marriage to William of Austria on 4 March 1375.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199158_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199158-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The children's <i><a href="/wiki/Sponsalia_de_futuro" title="Sponsalia de futuro">sponsalia de futuro</a></i>, or "provisional marriage", was celebrated at <a href="/wiki/Hainburg_an_der_Donau" title="Hainburg an der Donau">Hainburg</a> on 15 June 1378.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20158_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20158-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199165,_93_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199165,_93-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001170_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001170-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The ceremony established the legal framework for the <a href="/wiki/Consummation" title="Consummation">consummation</a> of the marriage without any further ecclesiastical act as soon as they both reached the age of maturity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199165_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199165-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Duke Leopold agreed that Jadwiga would only receive <a href="/wiki/Treviso" title="Treviso">Treviso</a>, a town that was to be conquered from the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Republic of Venice</a>, as dowry from her father.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199164–65_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199164–65-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> After the ceremony, Jadwiga stayed in Austria for almost two years; she mainly lived in <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199193_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199193-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Catherine died in late 1378.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169_4-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001169-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Louis persuaded the most influential Polish lords to swear an oath of loyalty to her younger sister, Mary, in September 1379.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20158_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20158-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199171_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199171-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> She was betrothed to <a href="/wiki/Sigismund_of_Luxemburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Sigismund of Luxemburg">Sigismund of Luxemburg</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001170_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001170-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> a great-grandson of <a href="/wiki/Casimir_the_Great" class="mw-redirect" title="Casimir the Great">Casimir the Great</a>, who had been Louis's predecessor on the Polish throne.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199152_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199152-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> The "promised marriage" of Jadwiga and William was confirmed at their fathers' meeting in <a href="/wiki/Z%C3%B3lyom" class="mw-redirect" title="Zólyom">Zólyom</a> (now Zvolen in Slovakia) on 12 February 1380.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20158,_10_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20158,_10-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199172–73_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199172–73-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Hungarian lords also approved the document, implying that Jadwiga and William were regarded as her father's successors in Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199173_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199173-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A delegation of the Polish lords and clergy paid formal homage to Sigismund of Luxemburg as their future king on 25 July 1382.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199175_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199175-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201510_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201510-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The Poles believed that Louis planned also to persuade the Hungarian lords and prelates to accept Jadwiga and William of Austria as his heirs in Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001170_17-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001170-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> However, he died on 10 September 1382.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001173_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001173-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga was present at her father's death bed.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199175_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199175-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Accession_negotiations_.281382.E2.80.9384.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Accession_negotiations_(1382–84)">Accession negotiations (1382–84)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Accession negotiations (1382–84)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_(crop).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_%28crop%29.png/220px-Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_%28crop%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="328" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_%28crop%29.png/330px-Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_%28crop%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_%28crop%29.png/440px-Louis%27s_kingdoms_and_his_vassal_territories_%28crop%29.png 2x" data-file-width="502" data-file-height="748" /></a><figcaption>Lands ruled or claimed around 1370 by Jadwiga's father, <a href="/wiki/Louis_the_Great" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis the Great">Louis the Great</a> (or the Hungarian): <a href="/wiki/Union_of_Hungary_and_Poland" title="Union of Hungary and Poland">Hungary and Poland</a> are colored <a href="/wiki/Red" title="Red">red</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Vassal_state" title="Vassal state">vassal states</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Naples" title="Kingdom of Naples">Kingdom of Naples</a> are coloured <a href="/wiki/Light_red" class="mw-redirect" title="Light red">light red</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Jadwiga's sister, Mary, was crowned king of Hungary five days after their father's death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199175_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199175-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001195_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001195-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> With the ceremony, their ambitious mother secured the right to govern Hungary on her twelve-year-old daughter's behalf instead of Mary's fiancé, Sigismund.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonter2012195_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonter2012195-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199197_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199197-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Sigismund could not be present at Mary's coronation, because Louis had sent him to Poland to crush a rebellion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201510_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201510-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> After he learnt of Louis's death, he adopted the title "Lord of the Kingdom of Poland", demanding oaths of loyalty from the towns in <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Poland" title="Lesser Poland">Lesser Poland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201510_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201510-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> On 25 November, the nobles of <a href="/wiki/Greater_Poland" title="Greater Poland">Greater Poland</a> assembled at <a href="/wiki/Radomsko" title="Radomsko">Radomsko</a> and decided to obey nobody but the daughter of the late king as she would settle in Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199199_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199199-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> On their initiative, the noblemen of Lesser Poland passed a similar agreement in <a href="/wiki/Wi%C5%9Blica" title="Wiślica">Wiślica</a> on 12 December.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199199_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199199-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Queen Elizabeth sent her envoys to the assembled lords and forbade them to swear an oath of loyalty to anyone other than one of her daughters, thus invalidating the oath of loyalty that the Polish noblemen had sworn to Sigismund on the late King Louis's demand.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199199_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199199-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Both Elizabeth's daughters had been engaged to foreign princes (Sigismund and William, respectively) unpopular in Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Polish lords who were opposed to a foreign monarch regarded the members of the Piast dynasty as possible candidates to the Polish throne.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201510_26-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201510-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Queen Elizabeth's uncle <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_the_White" title="Władysław the White">Władysław the White</a> had already attempted to seize Poland during Louis's reign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199166,_100_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199166,_100-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> However, he had taken <a href="/wiki/Monastic_vows" class="mw-redirect" title="Monastic vows">monastic vows</a> and settled in a Benedictine abbey in <a href="/wiki/Dijon" title="Dijon">Dijon</a> in Burgundy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201510_26-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201510-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Antipope_Clement_VII" title="Antipope Clement VII">Antipope Clement VII</a>, whom King Louis had refused to recognize against <a href="/wiki/Pope_Urban_VI" title="Pope Urban VI">Pope Urban VI</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199169–70_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199169–70-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> released Władysław from his vows, but he did not leave his monastery.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201511_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201511-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Meanwhile, <a href="/wiki/Siemowit_IV,_Duke_of_Masovia" title="Siemowit IV, Duke of Masovia">Siemowit IV, Duke of Masovia</a>, appeared as a more ambitious candidate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> He was especially popular among the nobility and townspeople of Greater Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20158_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20158-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100_32-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991100-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Queen Elizabeth's representatives released the Poles from their oath of fidelity that their representatives had sworn to Mary at an assembly in <a href="/wiki/Sieradz" title="Sieradz">Sieradz</a> in February 1383.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> The envoys also announced that she was willing to send Jadwiga to be crowned instead, on condition that she return to Buda after her coronation to live there until her twelfth birthday.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> The Polish lords accepted the proposal, but they soon realized that thereby the interregnum would be extended by a further three years.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101_36-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> At a new meeting in Sieradz, most noblemen were ready to elect Siemowit of Masovia king on 28 March.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101_36-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201515_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201515-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> They proposed that Siemowit should marry Jadwiga.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101_36-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> A member of the influential <a href="/wiki/T%C4%99czy%C5%84ski_family" class="mw-redirect" title="Tęczyński family">Tęczyński family</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Jan_T%C4%99czy%C5%84ski_(died_1405)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Jan Tęczyński (died 1405) (page does not exist)">Jan</a>, convinced them to postpone Siemowit's election.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991104_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991104-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The noblemen agreed to wait for Jadwiga until 10 May, stipulating that she was to live in Poland after her coronation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991104_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991104-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> They also demanded that <a href="/wiki/Dobrzy%C5%84_Land" title="Dobrzyń Land">Dobrzyń</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gniewk%C3%B3w" title="Gniewków">Gniewków</a> (two fiefdoms which her father had granted to <a href="/wiki/Vladislaus_II_of_Opole" title="Vladislaus II of Opole">Vladislaus II of Opole</a>), and "<a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Galicia%E2%80%93Volhynia" title="Kingdom of Galicia–Volhynia">Ruthenia</a>" (that had passed to Hungary in accordance with a previous treaty)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986202_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986202-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> be restored to the <a href="/wiki/Polish_Crown" class="mw-redirect" title="Polish Crown">Polish Crown</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki199171,_104_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki199171,_104-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Meanwhile, Jan Tęczyński and his allies, including <a href="/w/index.php?title=S%C4%99dziw%C3%B3j_Pa%C5%82uka&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Sędziwój Pałuka (page does not exist)">Sędziwój Pałuka</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://pl.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%99dziw%C3%B3j_Pa%C5%82uka" class="extiw" title="pl:Sędziwój Pałuka">pl</a>&#93;</span>, seem to have started negotiations with <a href="/wiki/Jogaila" class="mw-redirect" title="Jogaila">Jogaila</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_of_Lithuania" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duke of Lithuania">Grand Duke of Lithuania</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991103–104_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991103–104-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Siemowit's supporters however, tried to enter Kraków in the retinue of <a href="/wiki/Bodzanta" title="Bodzanta">Bodzanta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Gniezno" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Gniezno">Archbishop of Gniezno</a>, in May, but the townspeople closed the gates of the city before their arrival.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga had not arrived in Poland by the stipulated date (10 May).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Her mother's envoys stated that the spring floods had hindered Jadwiga's progress over the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_Mountains" title="Carpathian Mountains">Carpathian Mountains</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106_42-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Siemowit of Mazovia took up arms and advanced as far as <a href="/wiki/Kalisz" title="Kalisz">Kalisz</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106_42-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991106-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> His supporters assembled in Sieradz in August in order to elect him king, but Archbishop Bodzanta refused to perform his coronation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101,_106_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991101,_106-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> In a meeting in <a href="/wiki/Ko%C5%A1ice" title="Košice">Kassa</a>, Queen Elizabeth promised the delegates of the Polish provinces to send Jadwiga to Poland before November.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The queen mother and the Poles also agreed that if either Jadwiga or Mary died childless, her kingdom would pass to her surviving sister.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Siemowit having laid siege to Kalisz, Queen Elizabeth sent Sigismund of Luxemburg at the head of an "improvised army"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107_44-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> to Lesser Poland. Siemowit failed to take Kalisz, but news about the appalling behaviour of Sigismund's soldiers increased Sigismund's unpopularity in Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107_44-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Sędziwój Pałuka, who was the castellan of Kalisz and <i><a href="/wiki/Starosta" title="Starosta">starosta</a></i> of Kraków, led a delegation to <a href="/wiki/Zadar" title="Zadar">Zadar</a> in <a href="/wiki/Dalmatia" title="Dalmatia">Dalmatia</a> to negotiate with Queen Elizabeth, but she had him imprisoned instead.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107–108_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991107–108-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> She sent Hungarian soldiers to Poland to garrison them in <a href="/wiki/Wawel_Castle" title="Wawel Castle">Wawel Castle</a> in Kraków, but Pałuka escaped and successfully obstructed her soldiers entering the castle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At a <a href="/wiki/General_sejm" class="mw-redirect" title="General sejm">general assembly</a> in <a href="/wiki/Radomsko" title="Radomsko">Radomsko</a> in early March, the delegates of all the Polish provinces and towns decided to elect Siemowit king, if Jadwiga did not come to Poland within two months.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> They set up a provisional government,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108_46-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> stipulating that only the "community of lords and citizens" had the authority to administer Poland during the interregnum.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201516_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201516-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Queen Elizabeth, who was only informed of the decision by an informal message, realized that she could not any longer postpone Jadwiga's coronation and so sent her to Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108_46-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991108-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The exact date of Jadwiga's arrival is unknown, because the main source for the history of Poland during this period&#160;&#8211;&#32;<a href="/wiki/Jan_of_Czarnk%C3%B3w" title="Jan of Czarnków">Jan of Czarnków</a>'s chronicle&#160;&#8211;&#32; ended prior to this event.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991109_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991109-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Reign">Reign</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Reign">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span id="Coronation_.281384.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Coronation_(1384)">Coronation (1384)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Coronation (1384)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The interregnum that followed Louis's death and caused such internal strife came to an end with Jadwiga's arrival in Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJackson1999188_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson1999188-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991113_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991113-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> A large crowd of clerics, noblemen and burghers gathered at Kraków "to greet her with a display of affection",<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> according to the 15th-century Polish historian, <a href="/wiki/Jan_D%C5%82ugosz" title="Jan Długosz">Jan Długosz</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991113_50-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991113-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Nobody protested when Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Bodzanta" title="Bodzanta">Bodzanta</a> crowned her on 16 October 1384.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991109_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991109-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies200591_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies200591-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> According to traditional scholarly consensus, Jadwiga was crowned king.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201517_(note_38)_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201517_(note_38)-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Thereby, as Robert W. Knoll proposes, the Polish lords prevented her eventual spouse from adopting the same title without their consent.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKnoll201137_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKnoll201137-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Stephen C. Rowell, who says that sources that contradict the traditional view outnumber those verifying it, suggests that sporadic contemporaneous references to Jadwiga as king only reflect that she was not a <a href="/wiki/Queen_consort" title="Queen consort">queen consort</a>, but a <a href="/wiki/Queen_regnant" title="Queen regnant">queen regnant</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201517_(note_38)_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201517_(note_38)-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Bodzanta, Archbishop of Gniezno, <a href="/wiki/Jan_Radlica" title="Jan Radlica">Jan Radlica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Krak%C3%B3w" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Kraków">Bishop of Kraków</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dobrogost_of_Nowy_Dw%C3%B3r" title="Dobrogost of Nowy Dwór">Dobrogost of Nowy Dwór</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Pozna%C5%84" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Poznań">Bishop of Poznań</a>, and Duke Vladislaus II of Opole were Jadwiga's most trusted advisers during the first years of her reign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991114–115_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991114–115-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> According to a widely accepted scholarly theory, Jadwiga, who was still a minor, was "a mere tool" to her advisers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGromada1999434_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGromada1999434-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991114_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991114-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> However, Halecki refutes this view, contending that Jadwiga matured quickly and her personality, especially her charm and kindness, only served to strengthen her position.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991114_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991114-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Already in late 1384 she intervened on Duke Vladislaus's behalf to reconcile him with her mother's favourite, <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_I_Garai" title="Nicholas I Garai">Nicholas I Garai</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991116_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991116-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Refusal_of_William_.281385.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Refusal_of_William_(1385)">Refusal of William (1385)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Refusal of William (1385)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Polish lords did not want to accept Jadwiga's fourteen-year-old fiancé, William of Habsburg, as their sovereign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201517_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201517-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991116–117_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991116–117-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> They thought that the inexperienced William and his Austrian kinsmen could not safeguard Poland's interests against its powerful neighbours, especially the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Luxembourg" title="House of Luxembourg">Luxemburgs</a> which controlled <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Bohemia" title="Kingdom of Bohemia">Bohemia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Margraviate_of_Brandenburg" title="Margraviate of Brandenburg">Brandenburg</a>, and had a strong claim on Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201517,_33_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201517,_33-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991117_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991117-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> According to Halecki, the lords of Lesser Poland were the first to suggest that Jadwiga should marry the pagan duke Jogaila of Lithuania.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991118_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991118-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Jogaila sent his envoys&#160;&#8211;&#32;including his brother, <a href="/wiki/Skirgaila" title="Skirgaila">Skirgaila</a>, and a German burgher from <a href="/wiki/Riga" title="Riga">Riga</a>, Hanul&#160;&#8211;&#32; to Kraków to request Jadwiga's hand in January 1385.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201517,_33_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201517,_33-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991121–123_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991121–123-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga refused to answer, stating only that her mother would decide.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991123_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991123-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Jogaila's two envoys left for Hungary and met Queen Elizabeth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991123_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991123-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20153_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20153-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> She informed them that "she would allow whatever was advantageous to Poland and insisted that her daughter and the prelates and nobles of the Kingdom had to do what they considered would benefit Christianity and their kingdom",<sup id="cite_ref-Annals_y1385_p345_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annals_y1385_p345-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> according to Jan Długosz's chronicle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991127_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991127-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The nobles from Kraków, <a href="/wiki/Sandomierz" title="Sandomierz">Sandomierz</a> and Greater Poland assembled in Kraków in June or July and the "majority of the more sensible"<sup id="cite_ref-Annals_y1385_p345_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Annals_y1385_p345-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> voted for the acceptance of Jogaila's marriage proposal.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991129_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991129-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A bearded man on his knees by a young woman who stands at a door holding an axe" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg/220px-Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="239" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg/330px-Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg/440px-Dymitr_z_Goraja.jpg 2x" data-file-width="737" data-file-height="800" /></a><figcaption><i>Dymitr of Goraj</i> by <a href="/wiki/Jan_Matejko" title="Jan Matejko">Jan Matejko</a> depicts Jadwiga trying to break the castle gate to join William</figcaption></figure>
<p>In the meantime, William's father, Leopold III hurried to Buda in late July 1385, demanding the consummation of the marriage between William and Jadwiga before 16 August.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991127,_129_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991127,_129-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Queen Elizabeth confirmed the previous agreements about the marriage, ordering Vladislaus II of Opole to make preparations for the ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201534_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201534-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991130_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991130-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Canon_law_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon law (Catholic Church)">canon law</a>, Jadwiga's marriage sacrament could only be completed before her twelfth birthday if the competent prelate testified her precocious maturity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991130_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991130-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Demetrius_of_Esztergom" title="Demetrius of Esztergom">Demetrius</a>, <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Esztergom" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Esztergom">Archbishop of Esztergom</a>, issued the necessary document.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991131_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991131-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> William went to Kraków in the first half of August, but his entry to Wawel Castle was barred.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Długosz states that Jadwiga and William would only be able to meet in the nearby <a href="/wiki/Franciscan" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan">Franciscan</a> convent.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Contemporary or nearly contemporaneous records of the completion of the marriage between William and Jadwiga are contradictory and unclear.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132-135_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132-135-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201534_70-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201534-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> The official accounts of the municipal authorities of Kraków record that on 23 August 1385 an amnesty was granted to the prisoners in the city jail on the occasion of the celebration of the Queen's marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132–133_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991132–133-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> On the other hand, a contemporary Austrian chronicle, the <i>Continuatio Claustroneubuzgis</i> states that the Poles had tried to murder William before he consummated the marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991135_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991135-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> In the next century, Długosz states that William was "removed in a shameful and offensive manner and driven from the castle" after he entered "the Queen's bedchamber"; but the same chronicler also mentions that Jadwiga was well aware that "many people knew <span class="nowrap">that ...</span> she had for a fortnight shared her bed with Duke William and that there had been physical consummation".<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991134–135_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991134–135-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the night when William entered the queen's bedchamber, a group of Polish noblemen broke into the castle, forcing William to flee, according to Długosz.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991135_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991135-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> After this humiliation, Długosz continues, Jadwiga decided to leave Wawel and join William, but the gate of the castle was locked.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991135_76-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991135-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> She called for "an axe and [tried] to break it open",<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> but <a href="/wiki/Dymitr_of_Goraj" title="Dymitr of Goraj">Dymitr of Goraj</a> convinced her to return to the castle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991138_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991138-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201534_70-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201534-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Oscar Halecki says that Długosz's narrative "cannot be dismissed as a romantic legend";<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991139_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991139-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Robert_I._Frost" title="Robert I. Frost">Robert I. Frost</a> writes that it is a "tale, almost certainly apocryphal".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201534_70-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201534-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> There is no doubt, however, that William of Austria was forced to leave Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies200595_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies200595-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Marriage_to_Jogaila_.281385.E2.80.9392.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage_to_Jogaila_(1385–92)">Marriage to Jogaila (1385–92)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Marriage to Jogaila (1385–92)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg/220px-Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="302" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg/330px-Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg/440px-Psa%C5%82terz_florianski1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1640" data-file-height="2252" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Saint_Florian" title="Saint Florian">Saint Florian</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Psalter" title="Psalter">Psalter</a>, commissioned by Jadwiga in around 1370, held in the <a href="/wiki/National_Library_of_Poland" title="National Library of Poland">National Library of Poland</a> in <a href="/wiki/Warsaw" title="Warsaw">Warsaw</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Jogaila signed the <a href="/wiki/Union_of_Krewo" title="Union of Krewo">Union of Krewo</a> in August 1385, promising Queen Elizabeth's representatives and the Polish lords' envoys that he would convert to Catholicism, together with his pagan kinsmen and subjects, if Jadwiga married him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201547,_50_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201547,_50-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGromada1999434–435_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGromada1999434–435-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> He also pledged to pay 200,000 florins to William of Habsburg in compensation. William never accepted it.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201534,_47_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201534,_47-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Two days after the Union of Krewo, the <a href="/wiki/Teutonic_Knights" class="mw-redirect" title="Teutonic Knights">Teutonic Knights</a> invaded Lithuania.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991157_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991157-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Aeltere Hochmeisterchronik</i> and other chronicles written in the Knights' territory accused the Polish prelates and lords of forcing Jadwiga to accept Jogaila's offer.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991139–140_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991139–140-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> According to a Polish legend, Jadwiga agreed to marry Jogaila due to <a href="/wiki/Divine_inspiration" title="Divine inspiration">divine inspiration</a> during her long prayers before a <a href="/wiki/Crucifix" title="Crucifix">crucifix</a> in Wawel Cathedral.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991139_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991139-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Siemowit IV of Mazovia resigned his claim to Poland in December.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20154_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20154-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Polish lords' envoys informed Jogaila that they would obey him if he married Jadwiga on 11 January 1386.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201549_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201549-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991147_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991147-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Jogaila went to <a href="/wiki/Lublin" title="Lublin">Lublin</a> where a general assembly unanimously declared him "king and lord of Poland" in early February.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonter201274_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonter201274-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201549–50_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201549–50-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991150–151_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991150–151-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Jogaila went on to Kraków where he was baptized, receiving the Christian name, Władysław, in Wawel Cathedral on 15 February.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies200595_82-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies200595-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991152_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991152-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Three days later, 35-year-old Władysław-Jogaila married 12-year-old Jadwiga.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJackson1999190_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson1999190-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991152_94-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991152-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Władysław-Jogaila styled himself as <i>dominus et tutor regni Poloniae</i> ("lord and guardian of the Kingdom of Poland") in his first charter issued after the marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991155_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991155-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Archbishop Bodzanta crowned Władysław-Jogaila king on 4 March 1386.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost20154_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost20154-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Poland was transformed into a <a href="/wiki/Diarchy" title="Diarchy">diarchy</a>&#160;&#8211;&#32;a kingdom ruled over by two sovereigns.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991155_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991155-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga and her husband did not speak a common language, but they cooperated closely in their marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonter201274_91-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonter201274-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> She accompanied him to Greater Poland to appease the local lords who were still hostile to him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991160_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991160-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The royal visit caused damage to the peasants who lived in the local prelates' domains, but Jadwiga persuaded her husband to compensate them, saying: "We have, indeed, returned the peasants' cattle, but who can repair their tears?",<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> according to Długosz's chronicle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991160_97-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991160-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> A court record of her order to the judges in favour of a peasant also shows that she protected the poor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991160_97-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991160-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Pope_Urban_VI" title="Pope Urban VI">Pope Urban VI</a> sent his legate, <a href="/wiki/Maffiolus_de_Lampugnano" title="Maffiolus de Lampugnano">Maffiolus de Lampugnano</a>, to Kraków to enquire about the marriage of the royal couple.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991158_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991158-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Lampugnano did not voice any objections, but the Teutonic Knights started a propaganda campaign in favour of William of Habsburg.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991157–159_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991157–159-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Queen Elizabeth pledged to assist Władysław-Jogaila against his enemies on 9 June 1386,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991158_99-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991158-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> but Hungary had sunken into anarchy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986202_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986202-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> A group of <a href="/wiki/Slavonia" title="Slavonia">Slavonian</a> lords captured and imprisoned Jadwiga's mother and sister on 25 July.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001198_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001198-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The rebels murdered Queen Elizabeth in January 1387.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001198–199_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001198–199-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991164_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991164-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> A month later, Jadwiga marched at the head of Polish troops to Ruthenia where all but one of the governors submitted to her without opposition.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991165–166_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991165–166-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGromada1999435_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGromada1999435-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A crowned young woman on her knees with her hand on the Bible which is held by an old bearded man" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg/220px-Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="300" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg/330px-Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg/440px-Simmler_Queen_Jadwiga%27s_oath.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1638" /></a><figcaption><i>Queen Jadwiga's Oath</i>, by <a href="/wiki/J%C3%B3zef_Simmler" title="Józef Simmler">Józef Simmler</a>, 1867</figcaption></figure>
<p>Duke Vladislaus of Opole also had a claim on Ruthenia but could not convince <a href="/wiki/Wenceslaus_IV_of_Bohemia" title="Wenceslaus IV of Bohemia">King Wenceslaus of Germany</a> to intervene on his behalf.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991166_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991166-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga confirmed the privileges of the local inhabitants and promised that Ruthenia would never again be separated from the Polish Crown.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991166_106-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991166-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> After the reinforcements that Władysław-Jogaila sent from Lithuania arrived in August, Halych, the only fortress to resist, also surrendered.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986203_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986203-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> Władysław-Jogaila also came to Ruthenia in September.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986203_107-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986203-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> Voivode <a href="/wiki/Petru_II_of_Moldavia" title="Petru II of Moldavia">Petru II of Moldavia</a> visited the royal couple and paid homage to them in <a href="/wiki/Lviv" title="Lviv">Lviv</a> on 26 September.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986203_107-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeletant1986203-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> Władysław-Jogaila confirmed the privileges that Jadwiga had granted the Ruthenians in October.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991166_106-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991166-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> She also instructed her subjects to show the same respect for her husband as for herself: in a letter addressed to the burghers of Kraków in late 1387, she stated that her husband was their "natural lord".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonter201274_91-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonter201274-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991156_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991156-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On William's demand, Pope Urban VI initiated a new investigation about the marriage of Jadwiga and Władysław-Jogaila.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991167–168_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991167–168-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> They sent Bishop Dobrogost of Poznań to Rome to inform the pope of the <a href="/wiki/Christianization_of_Lithuania" title="Christianization of Lithuania">Christianization of Lithuania</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991170_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991170-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> In his letter to Bishop Dobrogost, Pope Urban jointly mentioned the royal couple in March 1388, which implied that he had already acknowledged the legality of their marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991170_110-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991170-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> However, <a href="/wiki/Gniewosz_of_Dalewice" title="Gniewosz of Dalewice">Gniewosz of Dalewice</a>, who had been William of Habsburg's supporter, spread rumours about secret meetings between William and Jadwiga in the royal castle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991170_110-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991170-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga took a solemn oath before Jan Tęczyński, stating that she had only had marital relations with Władysław-Jogaila.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991137,_180_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991137,_180-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> After all witnesses confirmed her oath, Gniewosz of Dalewice confessed that he had lied.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991180_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991180-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> She did not take vengeance on him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991180_112-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991180-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Strife_with_Sigismund_.281392.E2.80.9395.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Strife_with_Sigismund_(1392–95)">Strife with Sigismund (1392–95)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Strife with Sigismund (1392–95)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Jadwiga's brother-in-law, Sigismund, who had been crowned King of Hungary,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001199_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001199-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> started negotiations with the Teutonic Knights about partitioning Poland in early 1392.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991194_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991194-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga met Mary in <a href="/wiki/Star%C3%A1_%C4%BDubov%C5%88a" title="Stará Ľubovňa">Stará Ľubovňa</a> in May and returned to Kraków only in early July.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991195–197_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991195–197-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> She most probably accompanied her husband to Lithuania, according to Oscar Halecki, because she was far from Kraków till the end of August.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991198_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991198-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> On 4 August, Władysław-Jogaila's cousin, <a href="/wiki/Vytautas" title="Vytautas">Vytautas</a>, who had earlier fled from Lithuania to the Teutonic Knights, paid homage to Władysław-Jogaila near <a href="/wiki/Lida" title="Lida">Lida</a> in Lithuania on 4 August.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991198_116-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991198-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Negotiations between Sigismund and the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Master_of_the_Teutonic_Knights" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Master of the Teutonic Knights">Grand Master of the Teutonic Knights</a>, <a href="/wiki/Konrad_von_Wallenrode" title="Konrad von Wallenrode">Konrad von Wallenrode</a>, continued with the mediation of Vladislaus of Opole.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991200–201_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991200–201-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> However, Hungary's southern border was exposed to Ottoman incursions, preventing Sigismund from taking military measures against Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991207_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991207-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> Wallenrode died on 25 July 1393.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991211_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991211-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> His successor, <a href="/wiki/Konrad_von_Jungingen" title="Konrad von Jungingen">Konrad von Jungingen</a>, opened negotiations with the Poles.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991211_119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991211-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> During the discussions, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Boniface_IX" title="Pope Boniface IX">Pope Boniface IX</a>'s legate, John of Messina, supported the Poles.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991211_119-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991211-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png/220px-Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png/330px-Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png/440px-Stefan_wojewoda_moldawski_slubuje_wiernosc.png 2x" data-file-width="7211" data-file-height="7719" /></a><figcaption>Stephen I of Moldavia's promise of loyalty to Jadwiga and Jogaila against Sigismund</figcaption></figure>
<p>Jadwiga was a skilful mediator, famed for her impartiality and intelligence.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGromada1999435_105-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGromada1999435-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> She went to Lithuania to reconcile her brother-in-law, Skirgaila, with Vytautas in October 1393.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991199_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991199-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Relations between Poland and Hungary remained tense.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991214_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991214-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Sigismund invaded Moldavia, forcing <a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Moldavia" title="Stephen I of Moldavia">Stephen I of Moldavia</a> to accept his <a href="/wiki/Suzerainty" title="Suzerainty">suzerainty</a> in 1394.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991214_121-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991214-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Soon after the Hungarian troops left Moldavia, Stephen sent his envoys to Jadwiga and Jogaila, promising to assist Poland against Hungary, the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a> and the Teutonic Knights.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991214_121-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991214-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 17 May 1395, Mary died after a riding accident.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001201_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001201-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> According to the 1383 agreement between their mother and the Polish lords, Jadwiga was her childless sister's heir in Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991220_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991220-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Vlad_I_of_Wallachia" title="Vlad I of Wallachia">Vlad I of Wallachia</a>, a Hungarian vassal, issued an act of submission on 28 May, acknowledging Jadwiga and her husband as Mary's legitimate successors.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991221_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991221-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> The widowed king's close supporter, <a href="/wiki/Stibor_of_Stiboricz" title="Stibor of Stiboricz">Stibor of Stiboricz</a>, expelled Vlad from Wallachia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991222_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991222-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> Władysław-Jogaila gathered his troops on the Polish-Hungarian border, but <a href="/w/index.php?title=Eustache_Jolsvai&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Eustache Jolsvai (page does not exist)">Eustache Jolsvai</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://hu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jolsvai_Leust%C3%A1k" class="extiw" title="hu:Jolsvai Leusták">hu</a>&#93;</span>, <a href="/wiki/Palatine_of_Hungary" title="Palatine of Hungary">Palatine of Hungary</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Kanizsai&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="John Kanizsai (page does not exist)">John Kanizsai</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://hu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanizsai_J%C3%A1nos" class="extiw" title="hu:Kanizsai János">hu</a>&#93;</span>, <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Esztergom" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Esztergom">Archbishop of Esztergom</a>, stopped his invasion of Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001201_122-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001201-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991222_125-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991222-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> In September, Konrad von Jungingen told the <a href="/wiki/Prince-electors" class="mw-redirect" title="Prince-electors">prince-electors</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a> that the union of Poland, Lithuania, and Hungary under Władysław-Jogaila's rule would endanger Christendom.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991224_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991224-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> However, most of Sigismund's opponents, who were especially numerous in Croatia, supported the claim of <a href="/wiki/Ladislaus_of_Naples" title="Ladislaus of Naples">Ladislaus of Naples</a>, the last male member of the Capetian House of Anjou.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991223–224_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991223–224-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> On 8 September, the most influential Hungarian lords declared that they would not support any change in government while Sigismund was far from Hungary fighting against the Ottoman Turks.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991224_126-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991224-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Before the end of the year, peace negotiations between the representatives of Hungary and Poland ended with an agreement.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991225_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991225-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga adopted the title "heir to Hungary", but she and her husband took no further action against Sigismund.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991226–227_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991226–227-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Conflict_with_the_Teutonic_Knights_.281395.E2.80.9399.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Conflict_with_the_Teutonic_Knights_(1395–99)">Conflict with the Teutonic Knights (1395–99)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Jadwiga_of_Poland&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Conflict with the Teutonic Knights (1395–99)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The relationship between Lithuania and the Teutonic Knights remained tense.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991230–233_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991230–233-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga and her Polish advisers invited the Grand Master, Konrad von Jungingen, to Poland to open new negotiations in June 1396.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991233_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991233-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> Conflicts with Vladislaus of Opole and Siemowit of Masovia, who had not given up their claims to parts of Ruthenia and <a href="/wiki/Cuyavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuyavia">Cuyavia</a>, also intensified.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991234–235_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991234–235-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> To demonstrate that the territories were under Jadwiga's direct control, Władysław-Jogaila granted the <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Belz" title="Duchy of Belz">Duchy of Belz</a> (in Ruthenia) and <a href="/wiki/Cuyavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuyavia">Cuyavia</a> to her in early 1397.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991235_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991235-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> However, Jadwiga and her Polish advisers wanted to avoid a war with the Teutonic Order.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201589_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201589-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> In response, Władysław-Jogaila replaced most Polish "starostas" (aldermen) in Ruthenia with local Orthodox noblemen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201589_134-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201589-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> According to German sources, Władysław-Jogaila and Vytautas jointly asked <a href="/wiki/Pope_Boniface_IX" title="Pope Boniface IX">Pope Boniface IX</a> to sanction Vytautas' coronation as king of Lithuania and Ruthenia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost201589_134-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost201589-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Jadwiga and Jungingen met in <a href="/wiki/W%C5%82oc%C5%82awek" title="Włocławek">Włocławek</a> in the middle of June, but they did not reach a compromise.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991236–237_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991236–237-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> The Teutonic Order entrusted Vladislaus of Opole with the task of representing their claims to Dobrzyń against Jadwiga.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991237_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991237-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga and her husband met Sigismund of Hungary, who had returned there after his catastrophic defeat in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Nicopolis" title="Battle of Nicopolis">Battle of Nicopolis</a>, on 14 July.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991236,_238_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991236,_238-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> They seem to have reached a compromise, because Sigismund offered to mediate between Poland, Lithuania and the Teutonic Knights.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991240_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991240-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> On Jadwiga's request, Wenceslaus of Bohemia granted permission for the establishment of a college for Lithuanian students in Prague on 20 July 1397.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991247_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991247-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> Jadwiga, who had spent "many sleepless nights" thinking of this project, according to herself, issued a charter of establishment for the college on 10 November.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991247_139-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991247-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>She opened new negotiations with the Teutonic Knights, but Konrad von Jungingen dispatched a simple knight to meet her in May 1398.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991241_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHalecki1991241-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Władysław-Jogaila's cousin Vytautas also entered into negotiations with the Teutonic Knights because he wanted
